**Who migrates the launch, and when?** Anyone, as soon as it is `MigrationReady`, through the venue
the launch stores: `launch::migrate`, then `blast_migrators::cetus::migrate` or
`blast_migrators::bluefin::migrate` with the returned `Migration` in the same PTB, which only that
venue can redeem.

**What happens to unsold tokens and the sell burn?** Both are disposed of at migration: burned when
the supply is burn-only, or sent to `@0x0` when it is fixed. `blast_fun::events::Migrated` reports
`disposed_meme` and `total_supply_after`.

**Can the creator change the target, plan, fees, venue, tier, or LP-fee split after launch?** No; the
launch fixes them. Only the metadata (name, description, icon) changes, with the metadata cap. After
migration the curve-fee cap holder can propose a new split to the vault with an owner proof, after a
48-hour notice.

**How does the creator get paid?** The creator recipient claims a `CurveFeeCap<Meme>` once with
`launch::claim_creator_caps`, then calls `launch::claim_creator_fees` at any time for its share of
every trade fee. After migration it calls `launch::prove_owner` and
`blast_vault::vault::claim_owner_balances` in one PTB for the owner route's LP fees.

**How much of the LP fees does Blast take?** The `LaunchConfig.protocol_lp_fees` share applied at
migration, 1,500 BPS of each asset by default and at most 3,000, taken before the split. Migration
residuals also go to Blast.

**How does the opening round treat early buyers?** Every bid, including the creator's before share,
joins one pot that buys once at settlement, so all bidders pay the same average price. Each bidder
receives Meme and refunded Quote in proportion to its bid, rounded down, with the last delivery
taking the remainder.

**Can a launch fail or be refunded?** No. There is no cancellation, expiry, or refund: a launch that
never reaches its target stays `Live`, and holders exit by selling to the curve.

## Cetus and Bluefin venues

> **Deployment status:** `blast_migrators` is not yet published on mainnet; its interface may change before publication.

### What it is

Package `blast_migrators` holds the two venues a Blast sale can migrate to: Cetus
(`blast_migrators::cetus`) and Bluefin (`blast_migrators::bluefin`). Both products use them:
launchpad launches and presales. A venue takes part in a sale twice. At creation its `prepare` call
records the coin's pool tier and returns a sealed `blast_core::migration::VenueTicket` that the
product's `new` (for a launch, `launch::new`) redeems, so the sale can migrate only to that venue.
When the sale is ready, one PTB calls the product's own `migrate` (for a launch, `launch::migrate`),
which returns the sealed `blast_core::migration::Migration`, and then the venue's `migrate`, which
redeems it, creates a new full-range pool seeded with the sale's Meme and Quote, and locks the
position in a new shared `blast_vault::vault::Vault`, which has no removal path. Afterwards anyone
collects the position's trading fees through the venue; the vault pays Blast its share and splits
the rest by the vault's fee split. The vault itself (fee split, owner claims, partner payouts,
holder rewards) is described in the vault guide; this guide covers what the venues do and what each
call needs. The package imports no product and no product imports it.

### Objects and authority

#### Shared objects

| Object | How many | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `blast_migrators::venue_config::VenueConfig` | singleton | Venue-owned records, written once per coin and never removed: each Cetus coin's `PoolCreationCap` and tick spacing, and each Bluefin coin's tier. |
| `blast_admin::package_version::PackageVersion` | singleton, typed `PackageVersion<BLAST_MIGRATORS>` by `blast_migrators::blast_migrators::BLAST_MIGRATORS` | This package's version; every venue call checks it first. It defines no pause bits. |
| `blast_admin::package_admin::PackageAdmin` | singleton, typed `PackageAdmin<BLAST_MIGRATORS>` | Sealed custody of this package's `Publisher` and `UpgradeCap`, in Blast's generic package administration. |
| `blast_vault::vault::Vault<Meme, Quote>` | one per migrated sale | Created and shared by `migrate`; holds the venue position and the fee balances (vault guide). |

Calls also take objects from other packages:

- The product: at creation the creator passes its owned `TreasuryCap<Meme>` to `prepare`; at
  migration the venue takes the `blast_core::migration::Migration<Meme, Quote>` value the product's
  `migrate` returned earlier in the PTB, and no product object.
- Vault: `blast_vault_package::VaultRegistry` (migrate only), the vault package's
  `blast_admin::package_version::PackageVersion` (typed by `blast_vault_package::BLAST_VAULT_PACKAGE`),
  and the shared `blast_core::blast_treasury::Treasury` that Blast's LP fees go to.
- Sui: the Meme's Coin Registry `Currency<Meme>` (shared; mutable for collections and buybacks,
  which burn Meme; read-only in Bluefin `prepare` and `migrate`), the Quote's `Currency<Quote>`
  (Bluefin migration only), and `Clock` at `0x6`.
- Cetus: `GlobalConfig`, `Pools`, the pair's `Pool<CoinA, CoinB>`, and Cetus LP Burn's
  `BurnManager`, all shared.
- Bluefin: its `GlobalConfig` and the pair's `Pool<Meme, Quote>`, both shared.

#### Venue witnesses

`blast_migrators::cetus::Venue` and `blast_migrators::bluefin::Venue` are the venues'
sealed witnesses; only their own module constructs them. A venue's witness issues its tickets,
redeems its sales' migrations, keys its position inside the vault, and authorizes its fee
collections. Blast admits each witness type in each product's venue list (the launchpad's
`LaunchConfig`, so launches may start on it) and in the vault's `VaultRegistry` (so it may create
vaults), which must also list each product's package witness, for launches
`blast_fun::blast_fun::PackageWitness`, as the package witness that seals its sales' vault terms.

#### Who may call what

| Call | Who |
| --- | --- |
| `blast_migrators::cetus::prepare`, `blast_migrators::bluefin::prepare` | holder of the coin's `TreasuryCap<Meme>` (the creator), in the sale's creation PTB |
| `blast_migrators::cetus::migrate`, `blast_migrators::bluefin::migrate` | anyone, with the `Migration` the product's `migrate` returned in the same PTB |
| `blast_migrators::cetus::collect_fees`, `blast_migrators::bluefin::collect_fees` | anyone |
| `blast_migrators::cetus::settle_and_apply_fee_split`, `blast_migrators::bluefin::settle_and_apply_fee_split` | anyone, once the vault's proposed split is ready |
| `blast_migrators::cetus::compound_auto_lp`, `blast_migrators::bluefin::compound_auto_lp` | anyone |
| `blast_migrators::cetus::buyback_and_burn`, `blast_migrators::bluefin::buyback_and_burn` | Blast admin: `blast_core::blast_domain::BuybackAdmin` |
| `blast_admin::package_admin::schedule_upgrade`, `blast_admin::package_admin::authorize_upgrade`, `blast_admin::package_admin::commit_upgrade_and_advance`, `blast_admin::package_admin::schedule_make_immutable`, `blast_admin::package_admin::make_immutable`, `blast_admin::package_admin::schedule_upgrade_delay`, typed `<BLAST_MIGRATORS>` | Blast admin: `blast_admin::package_admin::UpgradeAdmin<BLAST_MIGRATORS>` |
| `blast_admin::package_admin::cancel_upgrade_action<BLAST_MIGRATORS>` | Blast admin: `blast_admin::package_admin::CancelAdmin<BLAST_MIGRATORS>` |
| `blast_admin::package_admin::wrap<BLAST_MIGRATORS>` | holder of the package's `blast_admin::package_version::Publication` and native `UpgradeCap` (once, at deployment) |

The vault's own calls (owner claims, fee-split proposals, partner payouts, holder rewards) are listed
in the vault guide; the owner acts there with an owner proof from the sale's product (for a launch,
`launch::prove_owner`). A Blast
admin signs in with `blast_acl::sign_in` on the shared `blast_acl::ACL` to get the role's
`blast_acl::AdminWitness`. Integrators cannot call admin functions.

#### Trust assumptions

- **Upgradeable package.** `UpgradeAdmin` can authorize compatible upgrades of this package after
  the upgrade notice (48 hours at publication). Positions are held by the vault package, under the
  venue's key: the Cetus migration liquidity is also burned through Cetus LP Burn, whose
  `CetusLPBurnProof` can only collect fees.
- **Venue operators.** Cetus's pool-manager role can create a reserved pair's pool without the
  coin's `PoolCreationCap`. If it does so first, Cetus migration aborts, and only a migrators
  upgrade could recover the sale. If Cetus stops registering the sale's tick spacing, migration
  aborts and the sale stays ready to migrate.
- **Auto-LP price-move skim.** Auto-LP adds at the pool's current price. A caller can move the price
  in the same transaction, compound, and move it back, skimming part of that call's auto-LP amount.
  Frequent compounding keeps each slice small. Buybacks are admin-only with a minimum output for
  the same reason.
- **Buyback timing.** The burn route's Quote waits until a `BuybackAdmin` acts; nothing forces a
  buyback.

### Lifecycle

1. **Prepare** (creation PTB): the venue records the coin's tier in `VenueConfig` and returns a
   `VenueTicket<Meme, Quote>`; the product's `new` redeems it and stores the venue. A coin is
   prepared once: its records are write-once.
2. **Sale**: the launch trades until `MigrationReady`, or the presale fills (product guides).
3. **Migrate**: in one PTB the product's `migrate` returns the sale's `Migration`, and the venue's
   `migrate` redeems it, creates the pool, and creates and shares the vault. A `Migration` cannot be
   dropped or stored, so a PTB that does not redeem it aborts, and a failed venue migration aborts the
   whole transaction, product step included: the sale stays ready to migrate.
4. **Collect**: any number of fee collections, fee-split changes, and buybacks through the venue.
   The vault never closes and its positions are never removed.

### Flows

Every venue call takes the migrators `PackageVersion<BLAST_MIGRATORS>` (shared) and checks it
before anything else. Calls that also mutate the vault take the vault package's version too, because
each package checks only its own version.

#### Prepare a coin on Cetus (creator, inside the sale's creation PTB)

`blast_migrators::cetus::prepare` (type arguments `<Meme, Quote>`) takes, in order: the migrators
`PackageVersion`, `VenueConfig` (mutable), Cetus `GlobalConfig`, Cetus `Pools` (mutable), the
`TreasuryCap<Meme>` (owned, by mutable reference), and `tick_spacing: u32`. It:

1. Checks that Cetus registers a fee rate for `tick_spacing` (Cetus aborts otherwise).
2. Mints the Meme type's single Cetus `PoolCreationCap` from the treasury cap and registers the pair
   at `tick_spacing`. Cetus aborts if it refuses the pair there.
3. Stores the cap under `blast_migrators::cetus::CreationCapKey` and the tick spacing under
   `blast_migrators::cetus::PoolTierKey` in `VenueConfig`, with no path to remove either, and
   emits `blast_migrators::events::CetusPoolReserved`.
4. Returns a `blast_core::migration::VenueTicket<Meme, Quote>` sealed by
   `blast_migrators::cetus::Venue`.

After this, only that cap (or Cetus's pool-manager role) can create the pair's Cetus pool at that
tick spacing, so no buyer can pre-create the migration pool at a price of their choosing. The fee
rate is whatever Cetus registers for the tick spacing.

#### Prepare a coin on Bluefin (creator, inside the sale's creation PTB)

`blast_migrators::bluefin::prepare` (type arguments `<Meme, Quote>`) takes, in order: the migrators
`PackageVersion`, `VenueConfig` (mutable), the `TreasuryCap<Meme>` (owned, by immutable reference,
proving the caller controls the coin), the Meme's `Currency<Meme>` (read-only), `tick_spacing: u32`,
and `fee_rate: u64`. It aborts with `blast_migrators::errors::EInvalidTier` unless `tick_spacing` is
in 1..=400 and `fee_rate` in 1..=20,000 (Bluefin's own pool-creation bounds, checked here because
Bluefin checks them only at pool creation), then with `blast_migrators::errors::ESymbolTooLong`
when the Meme symbol is longer than 32 bytes (the pool and its position store it). It stores a
`blast_migrators::bluefin::PoolTier` under `blast_migrators::bluefin::PoolTierKey` in `VenueConfig`,
emits `blast_migrators::events::BluefinPoolTierRecorded`, and returns a `VenueTicket<Meme, Quote>`
sealed by `blast_migrators::bluefin::Venue`. It needs no Bluefin objects.

Either way, pass the ticket to the product's `new` in the same PTB (for a launch, `launch::new`,
with the same `TreasuryCap` by value; launchpad guide, Flow 3). The ticket cannot be stored or
dropped.

#### Migrate to Cetus (anyone)

Nothing requires a separate transaction, so the PTB that completes a sale can migrate too.

1. Work out Cetus's coin order. Cetus orders a pool's coins `CoinA` and `CoinB` by type name, and
   the venue checks the order with Cetus's own `is_right_order<CoinA, CoinB>` (in Cetus's `factory`
   module). Dev-inspect it, or dry-run `migrate` with either order: the wrong one aborts with
   `blast_migrators::errors::EInvalidCoinOrder`.
2. Call the product's `migrate` (for a launch, `launch::migrate`; launchpad guide). It returns the
   sale's `Migration<Meme, Quote>`.
3. Call `blast_migrators::cetus::migrate` with type arguments `<Meme, Quote, CoinA, CoinB>` and, in
   order: the `Migration` result of step 2 (by value), the migrators `PackageVersion`,
   `VenueConfig`, Cetus `GlobalConfig`, Cetus `Pools` (mutable), LP Burn `BurnManager` (mutable),
   `VaultRegistry` (mutable), the vault `PackageVersion`, `Treasury`, and `Clock`. It returns
   nothing; the caller receives no object.

Inside the venue call, in order:

1. Checks the coin order (`blast_migrators::errors::EInvalidCoinOrder`) and that `{CoinA, CoinB}`
   is exactly `{Meme, Quote}` (`blast_migrators::errors::EWrongCoinPair`).
2. Redeems the `Migration` with its witness, receiving the pool's Meme and Quote and the vault's
   terms; a hand-off sealed for any other venue aborts.
3. Reads the coin's tick spacing from `VenueConfig` and Cetus's full-range ticks for it.
4. Computes the opening price from those atomic amounts with
   `blast_core::migration::sqrt_price_x64` (see Economics), creates the pool with the reserved
   `PoolCreationCap` at the recorded tick spacing, and seeds full-range liquidity with exactly the
   handed-off balances. It never adopts an existing pool.
5. Burns the position through Cetus LP Burn; Cetus records the liquidity as permanently locked.
6. Creates the vault from the terms with `blast_vault::vault::new` (aborts unless the registry
   admits `blast_migrators::cetus::Venue` and the terms' package witness), attaches the `CetusLPBurnProof`
   and Cetus's leftover coins with `blast_vault::vault::share_with_position`, and shares the vault.
   The leftovers are migration residuals, settled by the terms.

#### Migrate to Bluefin (anyone)

Bluefin pools always have Meme as coin A: `Pool<Meme, Quote>`.

1. Split a `Coin<SUI>` worth exactly the SUI pool-creation fee Bluefin currently configures. Bluefin
   aborts on any other amount, and the whole coin is paid.
2. Call the product's `migrate`; it returns the sale's `Migration<Meme, Quote>`.
3. Call `blast_migrators::bluefin::migrate` with type arguments `<Meme, Quote>` and, in order: the
   `Migration` result (by value), the migrators `PackageVersion`, `VenueConfig`, `Currency<Meme>`,
   `Currency<Quote>`, Bluefin `GlobalConfig` (mutable), `VaultRegistry` (mutable), the vault
   `PackageVersion`, `Treasury`, the `Coin<SUI>` fee (by value), and `Clock`.

Inside the call: it redeems the `Migration` as above; reads the coin's tier from `VenueConfig`;
creates `Pool<Meme, Quote>` at the tier, named `MEME-QUOTE` from the two Coin Registry symbols, with
no Meme icon and the Quote's icon; opens a position across the widest tick range Bluefin's
configured bounds allow at that spacing; seeds it with the handed-off balances; creates the vault
with the position and Bluefin's leftovers (residuals, settled by the terms) and shares it; and
shares the pool. The caller receives no object, but Bluefin records the caller as the new pool's
manager, a Bluefin role limited to the pool's reward emissions and to handing the role on.

#### Collect LP fees (anyone)

Anyone may collect, as often as they like. A collection that finds no fees is valid.

- Cetus: `blast_migrators::cetus::collect_fees`, type arguments `<Meme, Quote, CoinA, CoinB>`,
  with the `Vault<Meme, Quote>` (mutable), migrators `PackageVersion`, vault `PackageVersion`,
  `Treasury`, `Currency<Meme>` (mutable), Cetus `GlobalConfig`, LP Burn `BurnManager`, and the
  vault's Cetus `Pool<CoinA, CoinB>` (mutable). It takes no `Clock`.
- Bluefin: `blast_migrators::bluefin::collect_fees`, type arguments `<Meme, Quote>`, with the
  `Vault<Meme, Quote>` (mutable), migrators `PackageVersion`, vault `PackageVersion`, `Treasury`,
  `Currency<Meme>` (mutable), Bluefin `GlobalConfig`, the vault's `Pool<Meme, Quote>` (mutable), and
  `Clock`.

Order of operations:

1. On Cetus, prove the coin pair (`blast_migrators::errors::EWrongCoinPair`). Check the pool is
   the vault's own (`blast_migrators::errors::EPositionPoolMismatch`).
2. Collect fees. Cetus: from the burned position through LP Burn, plus the auto-LP position's fees
   once it exists. Bluefin: from the migration position.
3. Split through `blast_vault::vault::split_fees`: Blast's share of each asset (rounded up) is paid to
   the `Treasury` at once, or held for the partner when the sale named one; the rest is apportioned
   over the vault's fee split. Address routes are paid as coins; the owner, auto-LP, and
   holder-reward routes accrue in the vault; the burn route's Meme is burned and its Quote kept for
   buybacks. A zero collection moves and emits nothing.

Collection never adds liquidity: the auto-LP share waits in the vault for `compound_auto_lp`
below, so a venue refusing an add can never block collecting fees.

#### Compound auto-LP (anyone)

Anyone may compound, as often as they like, in the same PTB as a collection or apart from it. A
call that finds nothing the pool takes is valid and adds nothing.

- Cetus: `blast_migrators::cetus::compound_auto_lp`, type arguments `<Meme, Quote, CoinA, CoinB>`,
  with the `Vault<Meme, Quote>` (mutable), migrators `PackageVersion`, vault `PackageVersion`,
  Cetus `GlobalConfig`, the vault's Cetus `Pool<CoinA, CoinB>` (mutable), and `Clock`.
- Bluefin: `blast_migrators::bluefin::compound_auto_lp`, type arguments `<Meme, Quote>`, with the
  `Vault<Meme, Quote>` (mutable), migrators `PackageVersion`, vault `PackageVersion`, Bluefin
  `GlobalConfig`, the vault's `Pool<Meme, Quote>` (mutable), and `Clock`.

It checks the pool is the vault's own (`blast_migrators::errors::EPositionPoolMismatch`), takes every
waiting auto-LP Meme and Quote (every collection's part plus anything left from earlier calls), adds
what the pool takes at its current price, and keeps the rest in the vault
(`blast_vault_events::AutoLiquidityAdded`).

Auto-LP per venue:

- Cetus burned the migration position, so it cannot take liquidity. The first time both auto-LP
  balances are nonzero, the vault opens a second full-range position in the same pool (emitting
  `blast_vault_events::AutoLpPositionOpened`) and keeps it with no removal path. Each add fixes all
  of coin A if Cetus's liquidity math says the coin B balance covers it, else all of coin B if the
  coin A balance covers that, else adds nothing.
- Bluefin adds to the migration position. The venue picks the fixed side from the pool price and the
  position's bound prices, keeping a 2-atom margin on the other side (see Economics).
- Both add nothing while either side is empty, so a one-sided auto-LP balance waits until the other
  asset arrives.

#### Settle and apply a new fee split (anyone)

The owner proposes a split on the vault with an owner proof from the sale's product (for a launch,
`launch::prove_owner`), then `blast_vault::vault::schedule_fee_split` (product and vault guides). It
is ready after a fixed 48-hour notice. Once ready, anyone calls
`blast_migrators::cetus::settle_and_apply_fee_split` (`<Meme, Quote, CoinA, CoinB>`) or
`blast_migrators::bluefin::settle_and_apply_fee_split` (`<Meme, Quote>`) with the same arguments
as `collect_fees`, plus the `Clock` on Cetus. It checks the proposal is ready
(`blast_vault::vault::assert_fee_split_ready`), collects and splits every position's fees under the
current split, and then applies the new split (`blast_vault::vault::apply_fee_split`), so each fee
follows the split it accrued under. The call takes no split of its own: it applies only the stored
proposal.

#### Buyback and burn (Blast admin: `blast_core::blast_domain::BuybackAdmin`)

A `BuybackAdmin` calls `blast_migrators::cetus::buyback_and_burn` (`<Meme, Quote, CoinA, CoinB>`)
or `blast_migrators::bluefin::buyback_and_burn` (`<Meme, Quote>`) with the vault, the migrators
`PackageVersion`, the vault `PackageVersion`, the `AdminWitness` for `BuybackAdmin`,
`Currency<Meme>`, the venue's `GlobalConfig`, the vault's pool, `quote_in` (atomic Quote),
`min_meme_out` (atomic Meme), and `Clock`. It checks the pool is the vault's
(`blast_migrators::errors::EPositionPoolMismatch`), takes exactly `quote_in` of the vault's burn
Quote, swaps it for Meme in the vault's pool up to the widest price limit, burns the Meme bought (or
sends it to `@0x0` when the supply is fixed), keeps any Quote the pool did not take for the next
buyback, and emits `blast_vault_events::BuybackBurned`. The admin sets `min_meme_out` from an
off-chain price, because anyone can move the pool price inside a transaction.

#### Package upgrades (Blast admin: `UpgradeAdmin<BLAST_MIGRATORS>`)

The package uses Blast's generic package administration, typed by
`blast_migrators::blast_migrators::BLAST_MIGRATORS`. `blast_admin::package_admin::schedule_upgrade`
announces an exact 32-byte digest. After the notice, `blast_admin::package_admin::authorize_upgrade`
returns its upgrade ticket, and `blast_admin::package_admin::commit_upgrade_and_advance` commits the
receipt and moves `PackageVersion<BLAST_MIGRATORS>` to the next version in the same transaction.
From then on every call to the old package's code aborts the version check, so integrators must
call the current package address; type names keep the original address. Upgrading this package
leaves the products and the vault on their own versions; an upgrade of the vault requires a
relink of this package. `blast_admin::package_admin::schedule_make_immutable` schedules the end of upgrades, which
`blast_admin::package_admin::make_immutable` executes only from this package's own code (a later
upgrade must add that wrapper);
`blast_admin::package_admin::schedule_upgrade_delay` changes the notice. The package defines no
pause bits: migration stops with the product's migration pause bit, and buybacks with the vault's
admin pause bit.

### Economics

All amounts are atomic units. BPS are out of 10,000.

#### Migration seed and residuals

The opening sqrt price is `blast_core::migration::sqrt_price_x64`,
`floor(sqrt(coin_b / coin_a) × 2^64)` from the handed-off atomic amounts: Quote/Meme when Meme is
coin A, Meme/Quote otherwise. Display decimals never enter it. The seed fixes
the side with more atoms (Meme on a tie), unless the venue's math says that would overdraw the other
side by rounding, in which case it fixes the other side. Whatever the venue does not take is the
migration residual, settled by the sale's terms: residuals to Blast are paid to the `Treasury` at
once, or held for the partner when the sale named one; residuals to the owner wait in the vault.
Position principal plus residuals equals the hand-off.

#### Blast's LP-fee share and the split

Each collection takes Blast's share of each asset, `ceil(gross × bps / 10,000)`, with the vault's
per-asset share. A sale's vault starts with the share its product sealed into the `Migration`
(for a launch, the `LaunchConfig.protocol_lp_fees` value applied at migration), at most 3,000 BPS; Blast can change one vault's share only after a
fixed 48-hour notice. The rest of each asset is apportioned over the fee split's routes by largest
remainder, so the routes always sum to what is left. This is separate from Blast's one-time
fees a product takes at migration. The vault guide has the details,
including partner payouts.

#### Auto-LP math

Auto-LP never swaps. It fixes all of one side and pays what the venue asks of the other at the
current price; the rest of the other side waits. Bluefin's side choice, with `P`, `L`, `U` the
current, lower, and upper sqrt prices (Q64.64), `a` the Meme balance, `b` the Quote balance, and a
2-atom margin: fix Meme when `liquidity = a × (P × U / 2^64) / (U − P)` is positive and below 2^128
and `ceil(liquidity × (P − L) / 2^64) + 2 ≤ b`; otherwise fix Quote when
`liquidity = b × 2^64 / (P − L)` is positive and `ceil(liquidity × 2^64 / P) − floor(liquidity ×
2^64 / U) + 2 ≤ a`; otherwise wait. A price at or outside the range adds nothing. Cetus uses Cetus's
own liquidity math with no margin.

#### Buybacks

A buyback spends exactly `quote_in` of the burn Quote; Quote the pool does not take returns to the
burn Quote. Every Meme bought is burned, or sent to `@0x0` under a fixed supply.

### Time rules

- Preparation happens only in the sale's creation PTB; the tier never changes afterwards.
- The venue adds no migration deadline: the product decides when its `migrate` may return a
  `Migration`, and its migration pause bit stops it.
- A proposed fee split waits a fixed 48 hours; once ready it stays ready until someone settles and
  applies it, or the owner cancels it. Until then collections use the current split.
- Collections have no deadline or cooldown.

### Reading state

- Which venue a sale uses: the sale's stored venue, the type name of
  `blast_migrators::cetus::Venue` or `blast_migrators::bluefin::Venue`.
- A Cetus coin's tier: the `u32` tick spacing in the `VenueConfig` dynamic field keyed by
  `blast_migrators::cetus::PoolTierKey` (typed by the Meme); its `PoolCreationCap` is the dynamic
  object field keyed by `blast_migrators::cetus::CreationCapKey`, whose ID matches
  `CetusPoolReserved`.
- A Bluefin coin's tier: the `blast_migrators::bluefin::PoolTier` (`tick_spacing`, `fee_rate`)
  in the `VenueConfig` dynamic field keyed by `blast_migrators::bluefin::PoolTierKey`.
- A vault's pool: `blast_vault::vault::pool_id`, or the `pool_id` in its `VaultCreated` event. The
  vault's type arguments name the Meme and Quote; the `VaultCreated` event's third type argument
  names the venue.
- The positions: dynamic object fields of the vault keyed by `blast_vault::vault::PositionKey` (the
  Cetus `CetusLPBurnProof` or the Bluefin `Position`) and, on Cetus, `blast_vault::vault::AutoLpPositionKey`
  (the auto-LP `Position`), each typed by the venue witness.
- Pending LP fees: dev-inspect `collect_fees` against the live vault and pool.

### Events

| Event | Emitted by | What to derive |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `blast_migrators::events::CetusPoolReserved` | `blast_migrators::cetus::prepare` | type arguments `<Meme, Quote>`, `pool_creation_cap_id`, `tick_spacing`: the Cetus pair is reserved |
| `blast_migrators::events::BluefinPoolTierRecorded` | `blast_migrators::bluefin::prepare` | type arguments `<Meme, Quote>`, `tick_spacing`, `fee_rate`: the Bluefin tier |
| the product's migration events | the product's `migrate`, earlier in the same PTB | recipients paid, Blast's one-time fees, pool amounts (product guides) |
| `blast_vault_events::VaultCreated` | `migrate` | `vault_id`, `pool_id`, `position_id`, `liquidity`, the sealed `terms`, residual amounts; type arguments `<Meme, Quote, Venue>` |
| `blast_vault_events::FeesCollected` | `collect_fees`, `settle_and_apply_fee_split` | gross amounts, Blast's cut, each route's amounts in route order |
| `blast_vault_events::AutoLpPositionOpened`, `blast_vault_events::AutoLiquidityAdded` | `compound_auto_lp` | Cetus's auto-LP position; liquidity added and what still waits |
| `blast_vault_events::BuybackBurned` | `buyback_and_burn` | `quote_spent`, `meme_burned`, burn Quote still waiting |
| `blast_core::blast_core_events::ProtocolFeesPaid` | migration and collections on a vault without a partner | Blast's residuals and LP-fee cut paid to the treasury |
| `delayed::DelayedApplied` | `settle_and_apply_fee_split` | keyed by `blast_vault::vault::FeeSplitKey` with the vault ID as `owner`: the previous and new split |

The migrators, vault, and core events are plain Sui events (not wrapped in an envelope), typed by
the pair's `Meme` and `Quote`. Cetus and Bluefin emit their own pool, position, and swap events in
the same transactions.

### Aborts

| Flow | Abort | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| any | code 9 in `blast_admin::package_version` | call the current package ID of the migrators, product, or vault package |
| Cetus prepare | Cetus aborts in its own modules | choose a tick spacing Cetus registers, for a pair it accepts |
| Bluefin prepare | 3 `blast_migrators::errors::EInvalidTier`, then 4 `blast_migrators::errors::ESymbolTooLong` | tick spacing 1..=400, fee rate 1..=20,000; a Meme symbol of at most 32 bytes |
| Cetus migrate, collect, buyback | 0 `blast_migrators::errors::EInvalidCoinOrder` (migrate), 2 `blast_migrators::errors::EWrongCoinPair` | pass the pool's coin order; `CoinA` and `CoinB` must be the sale's Meme and Quote |
| migrate | `sui::dynamic_field` code 1 reading the tier; 8 `blast_core::errors::EWrongVenue` in `blast_core::migration` | call the migrate of the venue the sale stores, with that sale's `Migration` |
| migrate | the product's own lifecycle or pause abort in its `migrate` (product guides) | wait until the sale is ready to migrate and its migration is unpaused |
| migrate | 1 `blast_vault::errors::EUnadmittedVenue`, 33 `blast_vault::errors::EUnadmittedPackageWitness`, 4 `blast_vault::errors::EZeroLiquidity` (vault) | the venue and the product's package witness must be listed in `VaultRegistry`; the seed must give positive liquidity |
| migrate | Cetus aborts when the pair's pool already exists or the tick spacing is no longer registered; Bluefin aborts on a wrong creation fee | the sale stays ready to migrate; pass the exact Bluefin fee |
| collect, settle, buyback | 1 `blast_migrators::errors::EPositionPoolMismatch` in the venue's module | pass the vault's own pool |
| settle and apply | 14 `ENoPending` or 13 `ENotReady` from Blast admin's `delayed` module | wait until the scheduled split is ready |
| buyback | 7 `blast_vault::errors::EBuybackQuoteOutOfRange`, 8 `blast_vault::errors::EBuybackBelowMinimum`; Blast admin `EPaused` when the vault's admin bit is set | spend a positive amount up to the burn Quote; re-quote `min_meme_out` |

### Pitfalls

- Calling `prepare` outside the creation PTB. The `VenueTicket` cannot be stored, so prepare and the
  product's creation calls (for a launch, `launch::new`, `launch::finalize`, and `launch::share`)
  share one PTB.
- Passing the `TreasuryCap` by value to `prepare`. Cetus takes it by mutable reference and Bluefin
  by immutable reference; the product's `new` takes it by value afterwards.
- Choosing a Cetus tick spacing Cetus does not register, a Bluefin tier outside its bounds, or a
  Meme symbol longer than 32 bytes on Bluefin: creation aborts, and the tier can never change after
  creation.
- Passing Cetus coin types in Meme/Quote order instead of the pool's order.
- Calling the product's `migrate` without the venue's `migrate` in the same PTB. Its `Migration`
  cannot be dropped, so the transaction aborts; pass it to the stored venue's `migrate`.
- Forgetting the Bluefin pool-creation fee coin, or passing a different amount.
- Expecting the burned Cetus position to grow: auto-LP goes to the second position.
- Expecting a ready fee split to apply by itself. Someone must call `settle_and_apply_fee_split`.
- Calling an old package ID after an upgrade.

### Q&A

**Which venue does a sale migrate to?** The one whose `prepare` produced the ticket the sale
redeemed; the sale stores that venue's witness type. Only that venue's `migrate` can redeem its
`Migration`.

**What is the PTB to start a launch on Cetus?** `blast_migrators::cetus::prepare` with the
`TreasuryCap` by mutable reference (returns the `VenueTicket`), `launch::new` with the ticket and
the `TreasuryCap` by value, the launch setters, `launch::finalize`, and `launch::share`, in that
order.

**What is the PTB to migrate?** Two calls: the product's `migrate` (for a launch, `launch::migrate`),
then the venue's `migrate` with the returned `Migration` as its first argument. They succeed or abort
together.

**Can anyone withdraw the migrated liquidity?** No. The vault holds the position under the venue's
key with no removal path, and the Cetus migration position is also burned through Cetus LP Burn.

**Who can migrate a sale?** Anyone, once the product's `migrate` accepts it. The PTB that completes
the sale can migrate in the same transaction.

**Why do Cetus calls take `CoinA` and `CoinB`?** Cetus orders a pool's coins by type name, so the
pool's types may be `Pool<Quote, Meme>`. The venue proves the pair matches the sale and retypes
balances between the two orders.

**Who receives the migration residuals?** Whoever the sale's terms name: Blast, paid to the
`Treasury` or held for the partner payout, or the owner, held in the vault.

**How often should LP fees be collected?** As often as useful; collection is permissionless and
cheap to retry. Compound as often, with `compound_auto_lp`; frequent compounding keeps the auto-LP
price-move exposure small.

**How does the creator change the LP-fee split?** With an owner proof from the product (for a
launch, the curve-fee cap and `launch::prove_owner`), call `blast_vault::vault::schedule_fee_split`
in one PTB. After 48 hours anyone applies it with the venue's `settle_and_apply_fee_split`, which
first settles fees under the old split.

**Does pausing a product stop collections?** No. A product's migration pause stops new migrations
only; fee collection never pauses, and buybacks stop only with the vault's admin pause.

**Why might a Cetus migration fail?** Cetus no longer registers the tick spacing, Cetus's
pool-manager role created the pair's pool first, the vault registry does not admit the venue or the
product's witness, or the product's migration is paused. The sale stays ready to migrate in every
case.

## Vault

> **Deployment status:** `blast_vault` is not yet published on mainnet; its interface may change before publication.

### What it is

Package `blast_vault` holds the one LP vault every Blast sale migrates into. A vault keeps a
venue's liquidity position with no removal path, so the liquidity is locked for good. It splits
every LP-fee collection: Blast's share first, then the sale's fee split across the owner, auto-LP,
holder rewards, burn, and address routes. It also pays holder-reward rounds by Merkle proof. Only
an admitted venue's code can create a vault or move its fees, and only from terms an admitted
package witness sealed; integrators reach a vault through the
venue's calls, and call the owner, round, and payout functions on it directly.

### Objects and authority

- `blast_vault_package::VaultRegistry` (shared singleton) lists the admitted witness types: the
  venues that may create vaults and the issuers whose sealed terms they may use (the launchpad's
  and the presale's package witnesses). A witness is admitted by its type, which only its own module
  can construct. A venue obtains terms only from `blast_core::migration::redeem`; it never accepts
  terms from its caller.
- `blast_vault::vault::Vault<Meme, Quote>` (shared, one per migrated sale) holds the position under
  the venue's key, the terms the sale sealed (`blast_core::migration::VaultTerms`), the fee split
  and Blast's share, each behind a notice, and the owner, auto-LP, holder-reward, and burn
  balances.
- `blast_vault::holder_rewards::Round<Meme, Quote>` (shared, one per published round) holds one
  round's holder budget, its Merkle root, the claimed-leaf bitmap, and an optional gas reserve.
- `blast_admin::package_version::PackageVersion<BLAST_VAULT_PACKAGE>` (shared) gates every call by
  code version and holds the admin pause bit.
- Owner authority is a `blast_core::owner_proof::OwnerProof` that the sale's product issues to its
  creator-authority holder in the same PTB. A vault accepts it only for its own sale and package
  witness.

Blast admins hold these roles: `VenueAdmin` admits and removes venue and package witnesses; `FeeAdmin` changes Blast's
per-vault share; `RewardsAdmin` schedules rounds and settles gas reserves; `BuybackAdmin` spends
the burn route's Quote on buybacks; `CancelAdmin` cancels any pending change.

### Lifecycle

1. A venue creates the vault with `blast_vault::vault::new`, unshared, from terms the sale sealed.
   It aborts unless the venue's witness and the terms' sealing witness are both admitted. Blast's
   share is at most 3,000 basis points per asset because `migration::vault_terms` refuses to seal
   more.
2. The venue attaches its position and shares the vault with
   `blast_vault::vault::share_with_position`, which settles the migration residuals and emits
   `blast_vault_events::VaultCreated`.
3. Each fee collection through the venue calls `blast_vault::vault::split_fees`.
4. Holder-reward rounds are scheduled, published, claimed, and closed (see Flows).

A vault never closes; its position cannot be removed.

### Flows

#### Claim the owner's balances

1. Call the sale product's owner-proof function to get an `OwnerProof` (cap holder: the sale's
   creator authority).
2. Call `blast_vault::vault::claim_owner_balances` with the vault (shared), the proof, and the
   package version. It returns a `Coin<Meme>` and a `Coin<Quote>` holding every owner fee and
   owner residual, and emits `blast_vault_events::OwnerBalancesClaimed`. Transfer or use both coins
   in the same PTB.

#### Change the fee split (owner)

1. Get an `OwnerProof`, then call `blast_vault::vault::schedule_fee_split` with the proposed
   `blast_core::fee_split::FeeSplit` and the `Clock` (`0x6`). It is ready after a fixed 48-hour
   notice.
2. Once ready, the venue settles fees under the current split and applies the new one with
   `blast_vault::vault::apply_fee_split`, so every fee follows the split it accrued under. Integrators
   check readiness with `blast_vault::vault::assert_fee_split_ready`.
3. The owner may cancel with `blast_vault::vault::cancel_fee_split`, even once ready.

#### Pay Blast's partnered fees

When the sale named a partner, Blast's cut is held in the vault. Anyone calls
`blast_vault::vault::pay_protocol_fees_with_partner` with the vault, the package version, the
treasury, and the partner (all shared). The partner receives its cut of each asset, the treasury the
rest, and `blast_core::blast_core_events::ProtocolFeesPaid` is emitted. Without a partner, Blast's
cut is paid to the treasury during each collection.

#### Claim a holder reward

1. Read the round's leaf for your address from Blast's published allocation: the index, the
   beneficiary, the Meme and Quote amounts, and the proof (a list of 32-byte siblings).
2. Call `blast_vault::holder_rewards::claim` with the round (shared), the package version, those
   values, and the `Clock`. Anyone may relay a claim; the coins always go to the committed
   beneficiary. It emits `blast_vault_events::RewardClaimed`.

#### Close a round

At or after its claim deadline, anyone calls `blast_vault::holder_rewards::close_round` with the
round, the package version, its vault, and the `Clock`. Every unclaimed balance and any unsettled gas
reserve returns to the vault's holder rewards, and `blast_vault_events::RoundClosed` is emitted.

#### Publish a scheduled round

Once a scheduled round's notice has passed, anyone calls
`blast_vault::holder_rewards::publish_round` with the vault, the package version, the treasury, and
the `Clock`. It reserves the round's budget from the vault, opens claims for the scheduled window,
shares the `Round`, and emits `blast_vault_events::RoundPublished`.

### Economics

- Each collection first takes Blast's share of each asset, rounded up
  (`blast_core::protocol_fees::take_cut`). The rest is apportioned over the fee split's routes by
  largest remainder (`blast_core::fee_split::apportion`), so the routes always sum to what is left.
- Address routes are paid at once. Owner, auto-LP, and holder-reward routes accrue in the vault.
  The burn route's Meme is burned when the supply is burn-only, or sent to `0x0` when it is
  fixed; its Quote is kept for buybacks.
- Migration residuals go to Blast or the owner, as the sale's terms say.
- A round's gas reserve is at most 10% of its Quote budget. It is reimbursed to the treasury once,
  measured and capped, with `blast_vault::holder_rewards::settle_gas_reserve` (Blast admin:
  `RewardsAdmin`); the rest returns to the vault at once.

### Time rules

- Witness admissions start at zero notice, so Blast lists its products and venues at launch while
  the package is still paused; Blast then raises the notice before unpausing. Afterwards each
  admission and notice change waits the applied notice. Removing a witness takes effect at once:
  a removed venue creates no more vaults, and a removed package witness's sales can no longer migrate.
- Fee-split and Blast-share changes always wait 48 hours; no call changes that notice.
- A round waits the vault's round notice, 2 hours at creation, before anyone may publish it.
- Claims are open while the clock is before the deadline; a round closes at or after it.

### Pause

The package is published fully paused. The admin pause bit stops Blast-share changes, buybacks,
round scheduling and publication, and gas settlement. It never stops venue admissions, fee
collection, owner claims, partner payouts, holder claims, round closing, or cancellations.
Unpausing with `blast_vault_package::unpause` runs only on the current version and requires a
nonzero witness admission notice and a nonzero core `blast_core::blast_treasury::Treasury` notice,
with no pending admission or change to zero; it takes the `Treasury` beside the registry.

### Upgrades and immutability

The vault is upgradeable through `blast_admin`'s generic administration and is to be made
immutable after its audit. An `UpgradeAdmin<BLAST_VAULT_PACKAGE>` schedules that with
`blast_admin::package_admin::schedule_make_immutable` and, once the 48-hour upgrade notice has
passed, executes it with `blast_vault::blast_vault_package::make_immutable` (the shared
`PackageAdmin`, the role witness, the `PackageVersion` and `Clock`). The call runs only on the
current vault version (code 9 from `blast_admin::package_version` otherwise) and then destroys
the `UpgradeCap`; no later upgrade or repair is possible.

### Reading state

- Find a sale's vault without an indexer: its object ID is derived from the shared
  `blast_vault_package::VaultRegistry` and the key `blast_vault::vault::VaultKey { package_witness, origin }`,
  whose BCS is the sealing package witness's type name as a length-prefixed `String` (ULEB128
  length, then the ASCII name, with the original package ID written without `0x`) followed by the
  sale's 32-byte ID: `0x2::dynamic_field::hash_type_and_key(registry_id,
  0x2::derived_object::DerivedObjectKey<blast_vault::vault::VaultKey>, bcs(key))`. The launchpad's
  sealing package witness is `blast_fun::blast_fun::PackageWitness` and the presale's
  `blast_presale::blast_presale_package::PackageWitness`. Each sale has exactly one vault; a
  second `blast_vault::vault::new` for the same terms aborts in `sui::derived_object`.

- `blast_vault::vault::pool_id`: the pool the vault's position belongs to.
- The venue's positions are dynamic object fields of the vault, under the venue's
  `PositionKey` and, once auto-LP has opened one, `AutoLpPositionKey`; only the venue's own code
  can borrow them, so read them as object fields.
- Events carry every balance change; index `FeesCollected` for per-route amounts.

### Events

- `blast_vault_events::VaultCreated`: vault, pool, position, liquidity, terms, and residuals.
- `blast_vault_events::FeesCollected`: gross amounts, Blast's cut, and each route's amounts, in
  route order.
- `blast_vault_events::AutoLpPositionOpened`, `blast_vault_events::AutoLiquidityAdded`: auto-LP.
- `blast_vault_events::BuybackBurned`: Quote spent and Meme burned. The vault measures both from
  the buyback receipt, as it measures `AutoLiquidityAdded` from the auto-LP receipt.
- Burned Meme leaves the supply when the Meme `Currency` is burn-only; when its supply is fixed
  it is sent to `0x0` instead. Both supply states are final, so an indexer reads the
  currency's supply state once to tell the two apart.
- `blast_vault_events::OwnerBalancesClaimed`: the owner's payout.
- `blast_vault_events::RoundPublished`, `blast_vault_events::RewardClaimed`,
  `blast_vault_events::GasReserveSettled`, `blast_vault_events::RoundClosed`: rounds.

### Aborts

- Vault creation: `unadmitted_venue`, then `unadmitted_package_witness`. Scheduling Blast's share above
  3,000 basis points per asset aborts with `invalid_protocol_bps`.
- A venue's buyback or auto-LP return: `wrong_vault` for another vault's receipt,
  `returned_exceeds_taken` when it returns more than it took.
- Owner claim with nothing accrued: `nothing_to_claim`. A proof for another sale or package
  aborts in `blast_core::owner_proof`.
- Holder claim: `claims_closed` past the deadline, `invalid_index`, `already_claimed`,
  `invalid_beneficiary` for `0x0`, `empty_claim` for zero amounts, and `invalid_proof` when any
  leaf value or the proof is wrong.
- Closing a round early: `claims_still_open`; with another vault: `wrong_vault`.
- Publishing: the scheduled round's notice must have passed, the treasury must still be the one it
  expected (`treasury_changed`), and the vault must still hold its budget
  (`insufficient_budget`).

### Pitfalls

- The coins from an owner claim must be consumed in the same PTB.
- An `OwnerProof` cannot be stored or dropped; create it in the PTB that uses it.
- A claim relayed by anyone still pays the beneficiary in the leaf, never the relayer.
- A leaf's beneficiary must be an account. A claim pays by coin transfer, so a beneficiary that is
  an object (a pool, a vesting schedule, another vault) could never receive it, and the claimed
  leaf never returns to the vault. Blast's allocation leaves object-held balances out; nothing on
  chain tells the two apart.

### Q&A

**Can anyone withdraw the liquidity?** No. The position is held under the venue's key with no
removal path; only the venue's code can borrow it to collect fees or add auto-LP.

**Who receives the burn route's Meme?** Nobody: it is burned when the supply is burn-only, or sent
to `0x0` when it is fixed.

**Do claims stop when Blast pauses the vault package?** No. Holder claims, owner claims, fee
collection, and round closing never pause.

**What happens to unclaimed rewards?** At the deadline anyone closes the round and they return to
the vault's holder rewards for later rounds.

**Can Blast change its share?** Yes, up to 3,000 basis points per asset, after a fixed 48-hour
notice and only for fees collected afterwards.

## Shared core

> **Deployment status:** `blast_core` is not yet published on mainnet; its interface may change before publication.

### What it is

Package `blast_core` holds the money building blocks every Blast launch product shares, each
defined once: a basis-points unit and its exact apportionment, outright or vested payouts, the LP-fee split, the ledger that holds Blast's fees and pays the partner's cut,
the one treasury address, Merkle proofs, and the sealed values that carry a sale's liquidity to its
venue and prove a sale's ownership to its vault. It holds no user funds of its own. Products and
vaults embed its values and call it; integrators read its events and build PTBs that pass its
values between product and venue calls.

### Units and payouts

- `blast_core::bps::Bps` is a rate from 0 to 10,000 basis points; `blast_core::bps::new` aborts
  above 10,000. `blast_core::bps::apply_up` applies it rounding up.
- `blast_core::bps::apportion` splits an amount over rates that sum to exactly 10,000 by largest
  remainder: every part takes its floor, an optional named part takes its ceiling, and the
  leftover units go one each to the largest remaining remainders, earlier parts first. The parts
  always sum to the amount.
- Every launch package uses one basis-points denominator, `blast_core::constants::basis_points_denominator`
  (10,000), and one cap on Blast's share of LP fees, `blast_core::constants::max_lp_fee_bps`
  (3,000 per asset). The bounds core's constructors enforce are public too:
  `blast_core::constants::max_routes` (20 routes per fee split) and
  `blast_core::constants::max_vesting_duration_ms` (2^63 ms). All four are public compile-time
  macros.
- A `payout::Recipient`, built with `payout::recipient`, is a nonzero destination address and
  optional `payout::VestingTerms` (from `payout::vesting_terms`); the zero address aborts.
  `payout::pay_or_vest` pays an amount to a recipient as a coin or, when it vests, locks the amount
  in an irrevocable, shared `blast_fun_linear_vesting::Vesting` schedule that starts at the payout
  and returns its ID. A zero amount sends and locks nothing. Each product keeps its own recipient
  lists and shares. `payout::destination` and `payout::recipient_vesting_terms` (called as
  `recipient.vesting_terms()`) read a recipient; `payout::cliff_delay_ms`,
  `payout::period_duration_ms`, and `payout::periods` read its terms.

### Fee splits

`fee_split::FeeSplit` is a list of up to 20 routes summing to 10,000. Each route has a positive
share and a `fee_split::Destination`: an address, the vault's owner, auto-LP, holder rewards, or
burn. No address and no special kind appears twice. `fee_split::apportion` divides one collection
across the routes in order.

### Protocol fees, partners, and the treasury

`protocol_fees::Ledger` holds Blast's Meme, Quote, and SUI fees for one launch, presale, or vault.
Only the module that owns it can create it, under its package witness, and every payment event
names that witness type, so a payment's origin is attributable.
`protocol_fees::take_cut` moves Blast's rounded-up cut of a fee into it. A ledger is bound at
creation to the partner its sale named, or to none. `protocol_fees::pay` pays everything to the
treasury and aborts when a partner is bound; `protocol_fees::pay_with_partner` first pays that
partner its cut of each asset through `blast_partners::blast_partners::pay`, then the treasury the
rest. Both emit `blast_core_events::ProtocolFeesPaid`; an empty ledger sends and emits nothing. Products expose these payouts as calls anyone
may make. `protocol_fees::balances`, `protocol_fees::package_witness`, `protocol_fees::origin`, and
`protocol_fees::partner_id` read a ledger. `protocol_fees::destroy` deletes one that holds nothing
and aborts while any fee is held, so its owner pays it out first.

`blast_treasury::Treasury` is a shared singleton holding the address Blast's fees go to. A Blast
admin with `blast_domain::TreasuryAdmin` schedules a new address with
`blast_treasury::schedule_destination`; anyone applies it with `blast_treasury::apply_destination`
once its notice has passed (48 hours from publication); a Blast admin with
`blast_domain::CancelAdmin` cancels it with `blast_treasury::cancel_destination`. Read the current
address with `blast_treasury::destination`, the notice the next change waits with
`blast_treasury::delay_ms`, and a scheduled change, as a `delayed::Proposal<address>`, with
`blast_treasury::pending_destination`. `blast_treasury::is_destination_ready` takes the `Clock` and
returns whether `blast_treasury::apply_destination` would succeed now: a change is scheduled, the
clock reads its `ready_at_ms` or later, and the clock plus its next notice still fits in a `u64`;
it returns false with nothing scheduled, before `ready_at_ms`, and after a cancel or an apply. The
treasury is never deleted: every ledger pays through it.

### Migration hand-off and owner proof

- Before a sale's supply is minted, its venue prepares the coin and returns a
  `migration::VenueTicket` for the sale's exact Meme and Quote pair, sealed by the venue's witness
  (`migration::issue_ticket`), so a ticket cannot start a sale of another coin or Quote. The venue
  keeps the pair's pool tier. The product's creation call redeems the ticket with `migration::redeem_ticket`
  and stores the venue.
- `migration::VaultTerms` are a vault's terms: the sale, the package witness that sealed them, the fee
  split, Blast's LP-fee shares, the partner, and whether residuals go to Blast or the owner. Only
  `migration::vault_terms`, under the issuing package's witness, builds them. It aborts when either of Blast's LP-fee shares exceeds 3,000 basis
  points, so every sealed term is within the cap.
- At migration the product creates a `migration::Migration` with `migration::new`, sealed by its own witness, holding the
  pool's Meme and Quote and terms the same package witness sealed. Only the stored venue can consume it,
  with `migration::redeem`; any other venue aborts.
- `owner_proof::OwnerProof` is a product's sealed statement that the caller holds the sale's
  creator authority, issued with `owner_proof::new`. A vault consumes it with
  `owner_proof::assert_terms`, which aborts unless it names
  the vault's package witness and sale.

None of these values can be dropped, copied, or stored, so the PTB that creates one must consume it.

Readers never consume them. `migration::ticket_venue` (called as `ticket.venue()`) reads a ticket's
venue. `migration::venue`, `migration::balances` (the Meme and Quote amounts), and
`migration::terms` read a migration; only `migration::redeem` releases its balances, and a copy of
its terms authorizes nothing, since an admitted venue never takes terms from its caller and each
sale has one vault. `migration::origin`, `migration::package_witness`, `migration::fee_split`,
`migration::protocol_bps`, `migration::partner_id`, and `migration::residuals` read terms;
`migration::is_protocol` and `migration::is_owner` read the `migration::ResidualPolicy` it
returns. `owner_proof::package_witness` and `owner_proof::origin` read
a proof.

### Pool seed price

`migration::sqrt_price_x64` derives the price a concentrated-liquidity venue (Cetus or Bluefin)
seeds a migrated pool at, from the migration's atomic Meme and Quote amounts:
`floor(sqrt(B / A) * 2^64)`, where B / A is Quote / Meme when Meme is coin A and Meme / Quote
otherwise. Display decimals never enter it, and the divisor amount must be positive.
`migration::is_seedable_price` says whether both venues can seed a full-range pool at that price:
it must lie within half of their shared sqrt-price range (from 2 x 4,295,048,016 to
79,226,673,515,401,279,992,447,579,055 / 2). A product uses it to refuse a sale that could never
migrate. Both are pure reads anyone may call.

### Merkle proofs

`blast_core::merkle::verify` checks a positional Blake2b-256 proof of a leaf digest at an index. Leaves hash
as `blake2b256(0x00 || domain || payload)` with `blast_core::merkle::leaf`, where each tree's
purpose names its own domain string; internal nodes hash as `blake2b256(0x01 || left || right)`;
an odd node at any level is its own sibling. Malformed roots, digests, or siblings,
an empty tree, an index outside it, or a proof of the wrong depth return false instead of aborting.

### Roles

`blast_domain` declares Blast's product roles for the shared ACL: `TreasuryAdmin`, `FeeAdmin`,
`ListingAdmin`, `VenueAdmin`, `RewardsAdmin`, `BuybackAdmin`, `CreatorAdmin`,
`ClaimSignerAdmin`, and `CancelAdmin`. Only Blast admins hold them.

### Errors

Every abort is a small integer from the package's numeric registry; the reference lists each code.
The abort names the module that raised it.

## Partner codes

> **Deployment status:** `blast_partners` is not yet published on mainnet; its interface may change before publication.

### What it is

A partner code lets an app that brings flow to Blast earn a share of the fee
Blast charges on that flow. Package `blast_partners` holds the codes for every
Blast product: Boosts, the launchpad (with its post-migration LP vaults), and
presales. A partner is one deal: a shared `Partner` object with a share of
Blast's fee (0% to 80%) and a recipient address. A company holds one
`PartnerCap` that controls the recipient of every deal registered under it.
Users never pay for a partner: a partner is only ever paid out of Blast's own
fee. Initial shares apply immediately. A later share or managing cap Blast
proposes applies only when someone applies it, at least seven days after the
proposal. The module never changes that notice and the package is published
immutable, so no one can shorten it.

### Objects and authority

| Object | Kind | Held by | Authorizes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `blast_partners::blast_partners::Partner` | shared (`key` only, never transferable) | nobody; created by `blast_partners::blast_partners::register` | read by products on every trade; mutated only by the calls below |
| `blast_partners::blast_partners::PartnerCap` | owned (`key, store`; transferable, wrappable) | the partner company | `blast_partners::blast_partners::set_recipient` on every `Partner` whose applied cap is this cap's ID |
| `blast_partners::blast_partners::PartnerAdmin` | role type in Blast's ACL | a Blast `AdminCap` granted this role | `blast_partners::blast_partners::register`, `blast_partners::blast_partners::schedule_share`, `blast_partners::blast_partners::schedule_cap`, `blast_partners::blast_partners::cancel_share`, `blast_partners::blast_partners::cancel_cap` |

`Partner` fields:

- `recipient: address`: where payments go. Set by the live cap's holder, applies
  at once.
- `share_bps`: the share of Blast's fee, in basis points (BPS, out of 10,000),
  at most 8,000. A `delayed::Delayed<ShareKey, u64>` value.
- `cap_id`: the ID of the one `PartnerCap` that may set `recipient`. A
  `delayed::Delayed<CapKey, ID>` value.

A `delayed::Delayed<K, T>` comes from Blast's shared administration package
`blast_admin` and has five fields in BCS order: `owner` (the `Partner`'s ID),
`key` (`blast_partners::blast_partners::ShareKey` or
`blast_partners::blast_partners::CapKey`, each a one-byte unit struct), `value`
(the applied value), `delay_ms` (the notice), and `pending: Option<Proposal<T>>`.
A `delayed::Proposal<T>` holds `value`, `delay_ms` and `ready_at_ms`. The applied
value is always `value`; a proposal changes nothing until it is applied, even after
`ready_at_ms`. Both notices are 604,800,000 ms (seven days) and stay fixed:
`blast_partners` exposes no call that changes them.

Who may call: *anyone* may call `new_cap`, `destroy_cap` (on a cap they hold),
`pay`, `apply_share`, `apply_cap`, `delay_ms`, `max_share_bps`, and the readers
under Reading state; the *cap holder* (the applied
`PartnerCap`) may call `set_recipient`; a *Blast admin* with the `PartnerAdmin`
role (through `blast_acl::sign_in`) may call `register`, `schedule_share`,
`schedule_cap`, `cancel_share`, and `cancel_cap`.
Each product keeps its own list of the partners it accepts, managed by that
product's admin.

### Lifecycle

**Cap.** `blast_partners::blast_partners::new_cap` mints an inert cap: it
controls nothing until Blast registers a partner under its ID. It is *live* for
a partner while it is that partner's applied cap. After
`blast_partners::blast_partners::schedule_cap` proposes a different cap and seven
days pass, anyone may call `blast_partners::blast_partners::apply_cap`; only then
does the old cap stop working for that partner.
`blast_partners::blast_partners::destroy_cap` deletes a cap, live or not;
destroying a live cap leaves its partners with no working cap until a cap Blast
proposes with `blast_partners::blast_partners::schedule_cap` is applied, at
least seven days later.

**Partner.** `blast_partners::blast_partners::register` creates and shares the
partner with the agreed share and managing cap in force immediately. Registration
needs no `Clock`; there is no initial waiting period. From then on:

1. Blast proposes a new share or cap; the applied value stays in force.
2. A different proposal replaces the pending one and restarts the full seven
   days, even when the old proposal was already ready. Proposing the applied
   value, or the value already pending, aborts.
3. Blast may discard a proposal with `blast_partners::blast_partners::cancel_share`
   or `blast_partners::blast_partners::cancel_cap`, even after it is ready.
4. From `ready_at_ms`, anyone may call `blast_partners::blast_partners::apply_share`
   or `blast_partners::blast_partners::apply_cap`, the partner included. Nothing
   changes merely because time passes. `blast_partners::blast_partners::is_share_ready`
   and `blast_partners::blast_partners::is_cap_ready` say whether that call would
   succeed now.

There is no delete: a partner exists forever, because launches, presales, and
vaults bind its ID for good and pay Blast's held fees only with it. To switch it
off, Blast proposes and then applies a zero share; products that name it keep
working and simply pay it nothing.

**Product acceptance.** Each product accepts a partner in its own shared config,
mapping the partner's ID to the time it stops being accepted (`u64::MAX` until
removed): a dynamic field keyed by `PartnerKey(partner_id)`, or, for the presale,
the `acceptance_ends_at_ms` table of a `blast_partners::partner_list::PartnerList`:

| Product | Config object and key | Admin |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Boosts | `blast_boosts_aftermath::blast_boosts_aftermath::Protocol`, `blast_boosts_aftermath::blast_boosts_aftermath::PartnerKey` | `RiskAdmin` role (`operations::add_partner`, `operations::remove_partner`) |
| Presale | `creation_policy::CreationPolicy`, its `partners` `blast_partners::partner_list::PartnerList` | core `FeeAdmin` role (`creation_policy::add_partner`, `creation_policy::remove_partner`) |

Adding applies at once (and cancels a pending removal). Removing sets the value
to `now` plus `blast_partners::blast_partners::delay_ms()`: the partner stays
accepted for seven more days. A partner is accepted while the field exists and
`now < value`.

**Shared acceptance list.** `blast_partners::partner_list::PartnerList` is the
same acceptance rule as one embeddable value, for a product to keep inside its
own config. The launchpad embeds one in `launch_config::LaunchConfig` (its
`partners` field), changed by the `FeeAdmin` role through
`launch_config::add_partner` and `launch_config::remove_partner`. It maps each partner's ID to the time, in Unix epoch milliseconds,
it stops being accepted (`u64::MAX` until removed), held in a `Table`, and
records the `owner` object that embeds it. It has no authority of its own: only
the embedding module can reach it mutably, so that product decides who may
change it.

- `blast_partners::partner_list::new` creates an empty list for an owner.
- `blast_partners::partner_list::add` accepts a `Partner` at once, cancelling a
  pending removal or accepting a removed partner again; it aborts when the
  partner is already accepted with no removal pending.
- `blast_partners::partner_list::remove` keeps a partner accepted until `now`
  plus `delay_ms()`; it aborts unless the partner is accepted now with no
  removal pending.
- `blast_partners::partner_list::assert_accepted` aborts unless the list
  accepts the partner now; `blast_partners::partner_list::is_accepted` returns
  the same answer as a `bool`.
- `blast_partners::partner_list::contains` says whether the list has an entry
  for a partner (accepted, removal pending, or removed);
  `blast_partners::partner_list::acceptance_ends_at_ms` returns that entry's end
  time and aborts in `sui::dynamic_field` without one;
  `blast_partners::partner_list::length` counts entries, removed ones included;
  `blast_partners::partner_list::owner` returns the embedding object's ID.
- `blast_partners::partner_list::destroy` deletes the list with every entry and
  emits `PartnerListDestroyed`. It ends every acceptance at once, without the
  notice, so a product destroys it only with the object that embeds it.

### Flows

#### Become a partner (company)

1. Call `blast_partners::blast_partners::new_cap` (*anyone*). It returns a
   `PartnerCap`; send it with a TransferObjects command to the address that will
   manage your deals (for example your multisig).
2. Give Blast the cap's object ID, the recipient address, and the agreed share.
3. Blast's partner admin, in one PTB: `blast_acl::sign_in` with type argument
   `blast_partners::blast_partners::PartnerAdmin`, passing the shared `ACL` and
   its owned `AdminCap`; then `blast_partners::blast_partners::register` with a
   reference to that `AdminWitness<PartnerAdmin>`, your cap ID, the recipient,
   and the share. The new `Partner` is shared; its ID is in the `PartnerUpdated`
   event and the transaction's created objects. Its initial share is active immediately.
   Registration also emits `delayed::DelayedCreated` for the share and for the cap.
4. Blast's admin for each product adds the partner to that product's list.

`blast_partners::blast_partners::register` accepts any ID as the cap. Confirm on
chain that the `Partner`'s applied `cap_id.value` equals your cap's ID; a wrong
ID can only be fixed by proposing a new cap and applying it seven days later.

#### Change where a partner pays you (cap holder)

1. Call `blast_partners::blast_partners::set_recipient` with the shared
   `Partner` (mutable), your owned `PartnerCap` (by reference), and the new
   address. It takes no `Clock`.
2. It applies at once and emits `PartnerUpdated`. One call per partner.

#### Change a share or replace a lost cap (Blast admin)

1. `blast_acl::sign_in` with `blast_partners::blast_partners::PartnerAdmin`.
2. `blast_partners::blast_partners::schedule_share` (new share) or
   `blast_partners::blast_partners::schedule_cap` (new cap ID), with the shared
   `Partner`, the witness, the value, and `Clock`.
3. Each emits `delayed::DelayedScheduled` (after `delayed::DelayedCancelled`
   when it replaces a pending proposal) with `ready_at_ms`, seven days later.
4. From `ready_at_ms`, *anyone* calls `blast_partners::blast_partners::apply_share`
   or `blast_partners::blast_partners::apply_cap` with the shared `Partner`
   (mutable) and `Clock` at `0x6`; each emits `delayed::DelayedApplied`. Until a
   cap change is applied, the old cap keeps working, including for a thief
   holding it.
5. Instead, Blast may sign in and call `blast_partners::blast_partners::cancel_share`
   or `blast_partners::blast_partners::cancel_cap` (no `Clock`), even after
   readiness; each emits `delayed::DelayedCancelled`.

#### Pay a partner (products, or anyone)

`blast_partners::blast_partners::pay<T>` takes the shared `Partner` by immutable
reference, a `&mut Balance<T>` holding Blast's fee on a trade, and the
transaction context; it takes no `Clock`. It splits off `floor(fee * applied_share / 10,000)`, sends it to the partner's
`recipient`, emits `PartnerPaid<T>`, and returns the amount. The rest stays in
the caller's balance. With a zero share (or a fee too small to round above zero)
it sends nothing, emits nothing, and returns 0. Each positive payment creates
a `Coin<T>` and transfers it to the recipient with `public_transfer`. Integrators
never call `pay` directly; products call it inside their own partnered functions.

### How each product uses a partner

| Product | When the partner is named | Blast fee divided | Payment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Boosts | on each trade: `operations::mint_with_partner` or `operations::redeem_with_partner`, passing the `Partner` right after the `Protocol` | Blast's admin fee that trade retained; never the reserve fee | push: paid in the same call |
| Launchpad | once, at creation: `launch::set_partner` on the `LaunchInitializer` before `launch::finalize`; fixed for good | Blast's whole fee ledger on the launch: the creation fee (SUI), Blast's share of every curve trade fee (Meme and Quote), and Blast's one-time migration fee | held in the launch; paid when anyone calls `launch::claim_protocol_fees_with_partner` |
| LP vault (launchpad and presale) | inherited from the launch or sale at migration, in its vault terms | Blast's LP fees on each collection, and protocol migration residuals | held in the vault; paid when anyone calls `blast_vault::vault::pay_protocol_fees_with_partner` |
| Presale | once, at creation: `blast_presale::blast_presale::set_partner` on the `PresaleInitializer` before `blast_presale::blast_presale::finalize`; fixed for good | Blast's raise fee (Quote) only; Blast's token fee is never divided | held at migration; paid when anyone calls `blast_presale::blast_presale::pay_protocol_fees_with_partner` (once) |

Rules common to every product:

- Naming a partner requires that the product accepts it at that moment;
  otherwise the call aborts. Launches and sales that already name a removed
  partner keep paying it.
- The claim and payment calls are *anyone*: both payees are fixed, so no caller
  chooses where money goes. Each checks that the `Partner` passed is the one the
  launch, sale, or vault named, and aborts otherwise.
- A launch that names a partner cannot use `launch::claim_protocol_fees`; it
  must use `launch::claim_protocol_fees_with_partner`.
- Held fees are divided at the share **applied when they are paid**, not when
  they accrued.
- The `Partner` is read by immutable reference, so naming a busy partner adds no
  contention of its own.

### Economics

- Partner amount per payment: `floor(fee * share_bps / 10,000)` in atomic units
  of `T`, where `share_bps` is the applied share when the payment executes.
  Blast keeps the remainder, including every rounding dust.
- `share_bps` is between 0 and 8,000 (80%). Blast always keeps at least 20% of
  its fee.
- Users, holders, creators, buyers, and pools receive exactly the same amounts
  with or without a partner.
- Example (Boosts): a mint retains a 15.00 USDC admin fee and the partner's
  share is 4,000 BPS. The partner receives 6.00 USDC; Blast keeps 9.00 USDC.
- Example (presale): Blast's raise fee is 2,000 USDC and the share is 4,000 BPS.
  The partner receives 800 USDC; Blast keeps 1,200 USDC and all of its token
  fee.

### Time rules

- Delay: `blast_partners::blast_partners::delay_ms()` returns 604,800,000 (seven
  days). It is a compile-time constant of an immutable package.
- The initial share and managing cap apply at registration, without a Clock.
- A proposed share or cap can be applied by the first transaction whose `Clock`
  reads `ready_at_ms` or later; it takes effect only when `apply_share` or
  `apply_cap` succeeds. A replacement restarts the full seven days.
- A recipient change applies in the same transaction.
- A product removal keeps the partner accepted until `now + delay_ms()`; a trade
  or creation at or after that time aborts.

### Reading state

Move callers read a partner with `blast_partners::blast_partners::recipient`,
`blast_partners::blast_partners::share_bps` (applied),
`blast_partners::blast_partners::pending_share_bps`,
`blast_partners::blast_partners::cap_id` (applied), and
`blast_partners::blast_partners::pending_cap_id`; each pending reader returns an
`Option<delayed::Proposal<T>>`. `blast_partners::blast_partners::is_share_ready`
and `blast_partners::blast_partners::is_cap_ready` take the `Clock` and return
whether `apply_share` or `apply_cap` would succeed now: a proposal is pending,
the clock reads its `ready_at_ms` or later, and the clock plus the seven-day
notice still fits in a `u64`. They return false with nothing proposed, before
`ready_at_ms`, and after a cancel or an apply. The share readers keep the field's name,
`share_bps`, the repository's term for a partner's share in basis points.
`blast_partners::blast_partners::max_share_bps` returns the 8,000 BPS ceiling
`register` and `schedule_share` enforce. Off chain, read the shared `Partner`
object's content over RPC, or replay the events below.

- Applied share: `share_bps.value`.
- Pending share: `share_bps.pending`, with its `value` and `ready_at_ms`;
  readiness does not clear it.
- Which cap controls the recipient: `cap_id.value`; its proposal is
  `cap_id.pending`.
- Whether a product's `PartnerList` accepts a partner now:
  `blast_partners::partner_list::is_accepted`.
- Whether a product accepts a partner: read the product config's dynamic field
  `PartnerKey(partner_id)`, or the entry for `partner_id` in its embedded
  `PartnerList` table; accepted while it exists and `now < value`.
- Which partner a launch, sale, or vault names: its `partner_id: Option<ID>`
  field; a vault's comes from its terms, recorded in
  `blast_vault::blast_vault_events::VaultCreated`.

### Events

Every event is emitted directly (no envelope).

- `blast_partners::blast_partners::PartnerUpdated`: emitted by
  `blast_partners::blast_partners::register` and
  `blast_partners::blast_partners::set_recipient`. Fields: `partner_id`,
  `recipient`. The latest one per `partner_id` gives the recipient.
- Share and cap lifecycle events come from `blast_admin`'s `delayed` module,
  generic in the key (`ShareKey` for the share, `CapKey` for the cap) and the
  value type (`u64` or `ID`), with `owner` set to the `Partner`'s ID:
  `delayed::DelayedCreated` (registration: initial value and notice),
  `delayed::DelayedScheduled` (proposal: value, notice, `ready_at_ms`),
  `delayed::DelayedCancelled` (a cancel, or a proposal a new one replaced) and
  `delayed::DelayedApplied` (previous and new value, notice). Replaying them per
  `owner` and key reconstructs the applied and pending terms; `blast_partners`
  emits no event of its own for them.
- `blast_partners::partner_list::PartnerAdded`: emitted by
  `blast_partners::partner_list::add`. Fields: `owner` (the object embedding
  the list), `partner_id`.
- `blast_partners::partner_list::PartnerRemoved`: emitted by
  `blast_partners::partner_list::remove`. Fields: `owner`, `partner_id`,
  `acceptance_ends_at_ms` (exclusive). Replaying both per `owner` gives that
  list's accepted partners.
- `blast_partners::partner_list::PartnerListDestroyed`: emitted by
  `blast_partners::partner_list::destroy`. Field: `owner`. From then on the
  owner's list accepts no partner.
- `blast_partners::blast_partners::PartnerPaid<T>`: emitted by
  `blast_partners::blast_partners::pay` when it sends a positive amount. Fields:
  `partner_id`, `amount` (atomic units of `T`). The product records the trade in
  its own event in the same transaction:
  `blast_boosts_aftermath::events::PartnerFeePaid` (Boosts),
  `blast_core::blast_core_events::ProtocolFeesPaid` (launch claims, LP vaults and presale fees).
  `blast_fun::events::LaunchCreated` and
  `blast_presale::events::PresaleOpened` record the partner a launch or
  sale names.

### Aborts

| Code | Constant | Raised by | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | `EInvalidRecipient` | `register`, `set_recipient` | use a non-zero recipient |
| 1 | `EShareAboveMax` | `register`, `schedule_share` | share at most 8,000 BPS |
| 2 | `EUnchanged` | `schedule_share`, `schedule_cap` (value equals the pending proposal), `set_recipient` (same recipient) | pass a different value; to restart a pending proposal with the same value, cancel it first |
| 3 | `ENotLiveCap` | `set_recipient` | use the partner's applied cap; a ready but unapplied cap is refused until `apply_cap` |
| 4 | `EPartnerNotAccepted` | `partner_list::assert_accepted`, `partner_list::remove` | name a partner the list accepts now |

The codes come from the package registry `blast_partners::errors`. Codes 0 to 3
are raised in module `blast_partners`; `partner_list::add` and
`partner_list::remove` also raise `EUnchanged` (adding an accepted partner, or
removing one whose removal is pending), and code 4 is raised in module
`partner_list`. The share and cap calls also
abort in module `blast_admin::delayed` with `blast_admin`'s error codes:
`ENotReady` (13, `apply_share` or `apply_cap` before `ready_at_ms`),
`ENoPending` (14, apply or cancel with nothing proposed), `EInvalidDelay` (15, a
`ready_at_ms` that would overflow) and `EUnchanged` (16, proposing the applied
value). A product call that names a
partner it does not accept, or passes the wrong `Partner` to a claim, aborts in
that product's module with that product's own error.

### Pitfalls

- `PartnerPaid` alone does not prove a Blast trade:
  `blast_partners::blast_partners::pay` is public, and anyone can pay a partner
  from their own balance. Pair it with the product event in the same
  transaction.
- Each positive payment arrives as an owned `Coin<T>` at the recipient address.
  Repeated payouts produce separate coins that the recipient can merge.
- On Boosts, check acceptance before building a partnered trade; otherwise call
  the plain `mint` or `redeem`.
- On the launchpad and presales the partner is fixed at creation. Forgetting
  `launch::set_partner` or `blast_presale::blast_presale::set_partner` cannot be
  fixed later.
- Held fees are divided at the share applied at payment time. An applied cut
  to zero also cuts fees accrued earlier but paid later.
- A ready proposal is not in force. Payments keep the old share and the old cap
  keeps working until someone calls `apply_share` or `apply_cap`; a partner
  waiting on a raise can apply it itself.
- Whoever holds the cap controls the recipient of every deal under it. A stolen
  cap can redirect payments until a `schedule_cap` replacement is applied.

### Q&A

**How much can a partner earn?** Up to 80% of Blast's fee on the flow that names
it, as agreed with Blast and set by `blast_partners::blast_partners::register`.
Users pay the same fee with or without a partner.

**When am I paid?** On Boosts, in the same transaction as each mint or redeem.
On the launchpad and presales, when anyone calls the product's `*_with_partner`
claim or payment call; you may call it yourself.

**In which asset am I paid?** In the asset of the fee divided: Boost collateral;
launch Meme, Quote, and SUI; presale Quote; LP fees in both pool assets.

**Can Blast change my share without notice?** No. Every share or cap change
waits seven days, visible on chain the moment it is proposed, and only then can
anyone apply it. The notice is fixed in immutable code.

**Can I change my recipient myself?** Yes, with your `PartnerCap` through
`blast_partners::blast_partners::set_recipient`; it applies at once.

**I lost my cap. What now?** Mint a new cap with
`blast_partners::blast_partners::new_cap` and ask Blast to call
`blast_partners::blast_partners::schedule_cap` for each of your partners. After
seven days anyone, you included, calls `blast_partners::blast_partners::apply_cap`;
until then the old cap still works.

**Can one company have several deals?** Yes. Each deal is its own `Partner` with
its own share and recipient; one `PartnerCap` can manage all of them.

**Can another app use my partner code?** Yes. The `Partner` is a public shared
object; any app can name it on a product that accepts it, and you earn on that
flow.

**How do I reconcile income?** Sum `PartnerPaid<T>` by `partner_id` and coin
type, and join each with the product event in the same transaction for trade
detail.

**Can I charge my own users an extra fee?** Not through `blast_partners`. Take
your own fee in your own PTB commands (split it from the user's coin and send it
where you like); Blast takes no cut of it.

## API reference: `blast_fun` (Launchpad)
